I know it’s just a tool I was trained to use, but in that moment it feels like I could hurt someone or cut myself badly. I know that sounds a bit dramatic, maybe, but that’s the part that gets to me.\n\nDoctor: Is it just the straight razor that makes you feel like you could hurt someone or yourself, or are there other objects or situations that trigger similar feelings?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly just the straight razor, yeah. I mean, I can use clippers and scissors and all that without really thinking about it.\n\nBut when the straight razor is open at my station… I know it’s a tool I was trained on, and I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it suddenly feels like I could hurt somebody or cut myself badly. That’s the feeling that hits me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel uncomfortable or anxious around other sharp objects, like knives or broken glass?\n\nPatient: Not really with knives or broken glass, no… at least not in the same way. I mean, I don’t love broken glass, but I think that’s normal.\n\nIt’s really that straight razor when it’s open at my station. I know it’s just a tool I trained with, and I can use clippers all day without a problem, but when that razor is sitting there open, it suddenly feels like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly. I know that probably sounds a bit much, but that’s what happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious in situations unrelated to work or the straight razor?\n\nPatient: No, not really… I mean, I don’t think so. Outside of work I’m usually fine.\n\nIt’s pretty specifically that moment with the straight razor. I know it’s just a tool I trained with, and I can use clippers all day without any issue, but when that straight razor is sitting open at my station, it suddenly feels like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly. I know that sounds a little intense, but that’s the best way I can describe it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this way about the straight razor every time you use it or try to use it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… pretty much every time it comes up, honestly. I try not to make a big deal out of it, but the second a client asks for a razor shave, or I see the blade flipped open, I get that reaction.\n\nMy chest tightens, my hands get sweaty, and it’s like my body wants to step back before I’ve even really thought it through. I know that probably sounds dramatic, but that’s what happens.\n\nDoctor: Does this feeling of anxiety happen only when you are at work with the straight razor?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. It’s really just at work when the straight razor is involved.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound like I’m scared of every sharp thing, because I’m not. I mean, I trained with it, and I can use clippers all day without thinking twice. But when that straight razor is open at my station, it just hits me like… what if I hurt the person in the chair, or cut myself badly? I know that probably sounds extreme, but that’s the situation where it happens.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.   },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Probably, yeah… I mean, I don’t want it to sound like I just can’t handle my job or something.

But I think if I wasn’t working as a barber, I wouldn’t be around an open straight razor at my station like that. That’s the part that gets me. I know it’s just a tool I was trained to use, and I can use clippers all day without any problem, but when the straight razor is sitting there open, it feels like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly.

So… yeah, I think it would be less if I wasn’t in that situation.

    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n10\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF40.1, F41.0, F48.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, doctor. Um… I’m here because work has gotten a bit difficult. I’m a barber, and I’ve been having a really hard time with the straight razor. I know that probably sounds strange, but I’ve started doing things to avoid using it—like switching clients, saying the blade isn’t around, or just putting it away in a drawer.\n\nAnd if I really can’t get out of doing a shave, I kind of… shake through the whole thing. It’s embarrassing, honestly. I just figured I should talk to someone before it gets worse, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious around your clients when you're not using the straight razor?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I’m usually okay with clients—talking to them, cutting hair, using clippers, all of that is fine.\n\nIt’s more when the straight razor is out and open at my station. I know it’s just a tool I was trained to use, but in that moment it feels like I could hurt someone or cut myself badly. I know that sounds a bit dramatic, maybe, but that’s the part that gets to me.\n\nDoctor: Is it just the straight razor that makes you feel like you could hurt someone or yourself, or are there other objects or situations that trigger similar feelings?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly just the straight razor, yeah. I mean, I can use clippers and scissors and all that without really thinking about it.\n\nBut when the straight razor is open at my station… I know it’s a tool I was trained on, and I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it suddenly feels like I could hurt somebody or cut myself badly. That’s the feeling that hits me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel uncomfortable or anxious around other sharp objects, like knives or broken glass?\n\nPatient: Not really with knives or broken glass, no… at least not in the same way. I mean, I don’t love broken glass, but I think that’s normal.\n\nIt’s really that straight razor when it’s open at my station. I know it’s just a tool I trained with, and I can use clippers all day without a problem, but when that razor is sitting there open, it suddenly feels like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly. I know that probably sounds a bit much, but that’s what happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious in situations unrelated to work or the straight razor?\n\nPatient: No, not really… I mean, I don’t think so. Outside of work I’m usually fine.\n\nIt’s pretty specifically that moment with the straight razor. I know it’s just a tool I trained with, and I can use clippers all day without any issue, but when that straight razor is sitting open at my station, it suddenly feels like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly. I know that sounds a little intense, but that’s the best way I can describe it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this way about the straight razor every time you use it or try to use it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… pretty much every time it comes up, honestly. I try not to make a big deal out of it, but the second a client asks for a razor shave, or I see the blade flipped open, I get that reaction.\n\nMy chest tightens, my hands get sweaty, and it’s like my body wants to step back before I’ve even really thought it through. I know that probably sounds dramatic, but that’s what happens.\n\nDoctor: Does this feeling of anxiety happen only when you are at work with the straight razor?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. It’s really just at work when the straight razor is involved.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound like I’m scared of every sharp thing, because I’m not. I mean, I trained with it, and I can use clippers all day without thinking twice. But when that straight razor is open at my station, it just hits me like… what if I hurt the person in the chair, or cut myself badly? I know that probably sounds extreme, but that’s the situation where it happens.\n\nDoctor: Does the thought of using a straight razor outside of work, such as at home, cause you the same level of anxiety?\n\nPatient: I guess… not really the same, no. I don’t really use one at home, so it’s hard to compare.\n\nIt’s more when it’s open at my station, with a person sitting there, that my mind goes to that place — like I’m about to cut them or cut myself badly. I know it’s just a tool, and I was trained on it, so I feel a little embarrassed saying it like that… but that’s when it feels intense.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to use a straight razor in a non-work setting, such as shaving a friend?\n\nPatient: I think… yeah, probably, if it was actually a straight razor and I was shaving a friend. Maybe not exactly the same as work, but I’d still get that feeling.\n\nI know it’s just a tool, and I was trained with it. I can use clippers all day and it doesn’t bother me. But when the straight razor is open and there’s a person there, I start feeling like I’m about to hurt them or cut myself badly. I know that might sound like I’m overreacting, but that’s honestly where my mind goes.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about using a straight razor be less if you were no longer working as a barber?\n\nPatient: Probably, yeah… I mean, I don’t want it to sound like I just can’t handle my job or something.\n\nBut I think if I wasn’t working as a barber, I wouldn’t be around an open straight razor at my station like that. That’s the part that gets me. I know it’s just a tool I was trained to use, and I can use clippers all day without any problem, but when the straight razor is sitting there open, it feels like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly.\n\nSo… yeah, I think it would be less if I wasn’t in that situation.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I just figured I should talk to someone before it gets worse, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious around your clients when you're not using the straight razor?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I’m usually okay with clients—talking to them, cutting hair, using clippers, all of that is fine.\n\nIt’s more when the straight razor is out and open at my station. I know it’s just a tool I was trained to use, but in that moment it feels like I could hurt someone or cut myself badly. I know that sounds a bit dramatic, maybe, but that’s the part that gets to me.\n\nDoctor: Is it just the straight razor that makes you feel like you could hurt someone or yourself, or are there other objects or situations that trigger similar feelings?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly just the straight razor, yeah. I mean, I can use clippers and scissors and all that without really thinking about it.\n\nBut when the straight razor is open at my station… I know it’s a tool I was trained on, and I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it suddenly feels like I could hurt somebody or cut myself badly. That’s the feeling that hits me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel uncomfortable or anxious around other sharp objects, like knives or broken glass?\n\nPatient: Not really with knives or broken glass, no… at least not in the same way. I mean, I don’t love broken glass, but I think that’s normal.\n\nIt’s really that straight razor when it’s open at my station. I know it’s just a tool I trained with, and I can use clippers all day without a problem, but when that razor is sitting there open, it suddenly feels like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly. I know that probably sounds a bit much, but that’s what happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious in situations unrelated to work or the straight razor?\n\nPatient: No, not really… I mean, I don’t think so. Outside of work I’m usually fine.\n\nIt’s pretty specifically that moment with the straight razor. I know it’s just a tool I trained with, and I can use clippers all day without any issue, but when that straight razor is sitting open at my station, it suddenly feels like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly. I know that sounds a little intense, but that’s the best way I can describe it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this way about the straight razor every time you use it or try to use it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… pretty much every time it comes up, honestly. I try not to make a big deal out of it, but the second a client asks for a razor shave, or I see the blade flipped open, I get that reaction.\n\nMy chest tightens, my hands get sweaty, and it’s like my body wants to step back before I’ve even really thought it through. I know that probably sounds dramatic, but that’s what happens.\n\nDoctor: Does this feeling of anxiety happen only when you are at work with the straight razor?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. It’s really just at work when the straight razor is involved.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound like I’m scared of every sharp thing, because I’m not. I mean, I trained with it, and I can use clippers all day without thinking twice. But when that straight razor is open at my station, it just hits me like… what if I hurt the person in the chair, or cut myself badly? I know that probably sounds extreme, but that’s the situation where it happens.\n\nDoctor: Does the thought of using a straight razor outside of work, such as at home, cause you the same level of anxiety?\n\nPatient: I guess… not really the same, no. I don’t really use one at home, so it’s hard to compare.\n\nIt’s more when it’s open at my station, with a person sitting there, that my mind goes to that place — like I’m about to cut them or cut myself badly. I know it’s just a tool, and I was trained on it, so I feel a little embarrassed saying it like that… but that’s when it feels intense.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to use a straight razor in a non-work setting, such as shaving a friend?\n\nPatient: I think… yeah, probably, if it was actually a straight razor and I was shaving a friend. Maybe not exactly the same as work, but I’d still get that feeling.\n\nI know it’s just a tool, and I was trained with it. I can use clippers all day and it doesn’t bother me. But when the straight razor is open and there’s a person there, I start feeling like I’m about to hurt them or cut myself badly. I know that might sound like I’m overreacting, but that’s honestly where my mind goes.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about using a straight razor be less if you were no longer working as a barber?\n\nPatient: Probably, yeah… I mean, I don’t want it to sound like I just can’t handle my job or something.\n\nBut I think if I wasn’t working as a barber, I wouldn’t be around an open straight razor at my station like that. That’s the part that gets me. I know it’s just a tool I was trained to use, and I can use clippers all day without any problem, but when the straight razor is sitting there open, it feels like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly.\n\nSo… yeah, I think it would be less if I wasn’t in that situation.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel anxious about using a straight razor if you were in a completely different role or situation where you had to use it, but it wasn't related to barber work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I probably would, if I actually had to use it on someone.\n\nIt’s not so much the barber shop itself, I guess. I mean, I know it’s just a tool, and I was trained with it, and I can use clippers all day without thinking twice. But with the straight razor, when it’s open and there’s a person there, my mind kind of jumps to, like… I’m going to hurt them, or I’m going to cut myself badly.\n\nI know that sounds dramatic, maybe, but that’s the part that makes me anxious.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

It’s really just at work when the straight razor is involved.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound like I’m scared of every sharp thing, because I’m not. I mean, I trained with it, and I can use clippers all day without thinking twice. But when that straight razor is open at my station, it just hits me like… what if I hurt the person in the chair, or cut myself badly? I know it’s just a tool I trained with, and I can use clippers all day without a problem, but when that razor is sitting there open, it suddenly feels like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly. I know that probably sounds a bit much, but that’s what happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious in situations unrelated to work or the straight razor?\n\nPatient: No, not really… I mean, I don’t think so. Outside of work I’m usually fine.\n\nIt’s pretty specifically that moment with the straight razor. I know it’s just a tool I trained with, and I can use clippers all day without any issue, but when that straight razor is sitting open at my station, it suddenly feels like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly. I know that sounds a little intense, but that’s the best way I can describe it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this way about the straight razor every time you use it or try to use it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… pretty much every time it comes up, honestly. I try not to make a big deal out of it, but the second a client asks for a razor shave, or I see the blade flipped open, I get that reaction.\n\nMy chest tightens, my hands get sweaty, and it’s like my body wants to step back before I’ve even really thought it through. I know that probably sounds dramatic, but that’s what happens.\n\nDoctor: Does this feeling of anxiety happen only when you are at work with the straight razor?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. It’s really just at work when the straight razor is involved.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound like I’m scared of every sharp thing, because I’m not. I mean, I trained with it, and I can use clippers all day without thinking twice. But when that straight razor is open at my station, it just hits me like… what if I hurt the person in the chair, or cut myself badly? I know that probably sounds extreme, but that’s the situation where it happens.\n\nDoctor: Does the thought of using a straight razor outside of work, such as at home, cause you the same level of anxiety?\n\nPatient: I guess… not really the same, no. I don’t really use one at home, so it’s hard to compare.\n\nIt’s more when it’s open at my station, with a person sitting there, that my mind goes to that place — like I’m about to cut them or cut myself badly. I know it’s just a tool, and I was trained on it, so I feel a little embarrassed saying it like that… but that’s when it feels intense.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to use a straight razor in a non-work setting, such as shaving a friend?\n\nPatient: I think… yeah, probably, if it was actually a straight razor and I was shaving a friend. Maybe not exactly the same as work, but I’d still get that feeling.\n\nI know it’s just a tool, and I was trained with it. I can use clippers all day and it doesn’t bother me. But when the straight razor is open and there’s a person there, I start feeling like I’m about to hurt them or cut myself badly. I know that might sound like I’m overreacting, but that’s honestly where my mind goes.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about using a straight razor be less if you were no longer working as a barber?\n\nPatient: Probably, yeah… I mean, I don’t want it to sound like I just can’t handle my job or something.\n\nBut I think if I wasn’t working as a barber, I wouldn’t be around an open straight razor at my station like that. That’s the part that gets me. I know it’s just a tool I was trained to use, and I can use clippers all day without any problem, but when the straight razor is sitting there open, it feels like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly.\n\nSo… yeah, I think it would be less if I wasn’t in that situation.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel anxious about using a straight razor if you were in a completely different role or situation where you had to use it, but it wasn't related to barber work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I probably would, if I actually had to use it on someone.\n\nIt’s not so much the barber shop itself, I guess. I mean, I know it’s just a tool, and I was trained with it, and I can use clippers all day without thinking twice. But with the straight razor, when it’s open and there’s a person there, my mind kind of jumps to, like… I’m going to hurt them, or I’m going to cut myself badly.\n\nI know that sounds dramatic, maybe, but that’s the part that makes me anxious.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to use a straight razor on yourself, rather than on another person?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I would, at least some.\n\nMaybe it wouldn’t be exactly the same as doing it on another person, because then I’m worried about them too, you know? But if it’s an open straight razor and I’m using it on myself, I still get that thought like I could cut myself badly.\n\nI know it’s just a tool, and I was trained with it, so I feel a little embarrassed saying that. Clippers don’t bother me at all. It’s really that open straight razor that makes me tense.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety triggered by the act of shaving itself, or is it more about the straight razor being present?\n\nPatient: It’s more the straight razor being there, I think… not shaving in general.\n\nI mean, I can use clippers all day and I don’t really think about it. And I know the razor is just a tool I trained with, so I feel kind of silly saying it, but when it’s sitting open at my station, that’s when I get tense. It feels like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly.\n\nSo yeah… it’s not the act of shaving so much as that open straight razor.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your anxiety about the straight razor would decrease if you were able to master using it again and feel fully competent with it?\n\nPatient: Maybe it would help a little if I felt more confident again, yeah… I don’t want to say it wouldn’t.\n\nBut honestly, I already know it’s just a tool, and I was trained to use it. I can use clippers all day without thinking twice. It’s just when that straight razor is open and sitting there at my station, I get this feeling like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly.\n\nSo… I’d like to think mastering it would make it go away, but I’m not completely sure it’s only about skill, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety about the straight razor more about the possibility of it causing harm, rather than the act of using it itself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably right.\n\nIt’s not really shaving itself that gets me. I know it’s just a tool, and I was trained with it, so I almost feel like I shouldn’t be making such a big deal of it. But when the straight razor is open and sitting there at my station, my mind goes to, like… what if I hurt somebody, or what if I cut myself badly?\n\nSo yeah, I’d say it’s more about what it could do, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about the straight razor be present if you were to encounter it in a completely unrelated and non-threatening situation, such as seeing one in a museum?\n\nPatient: I think… probably not as much, if it was just in a museum behind glass or something.\n\nI mean, I know it’s just a tool, and I trained with it. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I think… yeah, it would probably still happen if it felt like the same kind of setup.

Like, I don’t think it’s only the shop itself. I know the razor is just a tool, and I was trained with it, and I can use clippers all day without any problem. But if I’m in a work-like setting and that straight razor is open and right there, it still feels like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly.

Maybe it wouldn’t be exactly as intense if it wasn’t my actual job, but… I think the fear would still be there. Is that what you mean?

I know it’s just a tool I trained with, and I can use clippers all day without any issue, but when that straight razor is sitting open at my station, it suddenly feels like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly. But when the straight razor is open and there’s a person there, I start feeling like I’m about to hurt them or cut myself badly. I know that might sound like I’m overreacting, but that’s honestly where my mind goes.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about using a straight razor be less if you were no longer working as a barber?\n\nPatient: Probably, yeah… I mean, I don’t want it to sound like I just can’t handle my job or something.\n\nBut I think if I wasn’t working as a barber, I wouldn’t be around an open straight razor at my station like that. That’s the part that gets me. I know it’s just a tool I was trained to use, and I can use clippers all day without any problem, but when the straight razor is sitting there open, it feels like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly.\n\nSo… yeah, I think it would be less if I wasn’t in that situation.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel anxious about using a straight razor if you were in a completely different role or situation where you had to use it, but it wasn't related to barber work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I probably would, if I actually had to use it on someone.\n\nIt’s not so much the barber shop itself, I guess. I mean, I know it’s just a tool, and I was trained with it, and I can use clippers all day without thinking twice. It’s just when that straight razor is open and sitting there at my station, I get this feeling like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly.\n\nSo… I’d like to think mastering it would make it go away, but I’m not completely sure it’s only about skill, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety about the straight razor more about the possibility of it causing harm, rather than the act of using it itself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably right.\n\nIt’s not really shaving itself that gets me. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Maybe somewhat, if it felt less serious or less like I was responsible for someone in the chair.

That’s the part that gets me. I know it’s just a tool I was trained to use, and I can use clippers all day without any problem, but when the straight razor is sitting there open, it feels like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly.\n\nSo… yeah, I think it would be less if I wasn’t in that situation.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel anxious about using a straight razor if you were in a completely different role or situation where you had to use it, but it wasn't related to barber work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I probably would, if I actually had to use it on someone.\n\nIt’s not so much the barber shop itself, I guess. I think at home shaving myself, it might be a little different… maybe less pressure than having a client there.

But honestly, if it’s the straight razor and it’s open in my hand, I think I’d still get that same kind of jolt. It’s embarrassing, honestly. I’m usually okay with clients—talking to them, cutting hair, using clippers, all of that is fine.\n\nIt’s more when the straight razor is out and open at my station. I know it’s just a tool I was trained to use, but in that moment it feels like I could hurt someone or cut myself badly. I know that sounds a bit dramatic, maybe, but that’s the part that gets to me.\n\nDoctor: Is it just the straight razor that makes you feel like you could hurt someone or yourself, or are there other objects or situations that trigger similar feelings?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly just the straight razor, yeah. I mean, I can use clippers and scissors and all that without really thinking about it.\n\nBut when the straight razor is open at my station… I know it’s a tool I was trained on, and I don’t want to make it sound worse than it is, but it suddenly feels like I could hurt somebody or cut myself badly. That’s the feeling that hits me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel uncomfortable or anxious around other sharp objects, like knives or broken glass?\n\nPatient: Not really with knives or broken glass, no… at least not in the same way. I mean, I don’t love broken glass, but I think that’s normal.\n\nIt’s really that straight razor when it’s open at my station. I know it’s just a tool I trained with, and I can use clippers all day without a problem, but when that razor is sitting there open, it suddenly feels like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly. I know that probably sounds a bit much, but that’s what happens.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious in situations unrelated to work or the straight razor?\n\nPatient: No, not really… I mean, I don’t think so. Outside of work I’m usually fine.\n\nIt’s pretty specifically that moment with the straight razor. I know it’s just a tool I trained with, and I can use clippers all day without any issue, but when that straight razor is sitting open at my station, it suddenly feels like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly. I know that sounds a little intense, but that’s the best way I can describe it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this way about the straight razor every time you use it or try to use it?\n\nPatient: Yeah… pretty much every time it comes up, honestly. I try not to make a big deal out of it, but the second a client asks for a razor shave, or I see the blade flipped open, I get that reaction.\n\nMy chest tightens, my hands get sweaty, and it’s like my body wants to step back before I’ve even really thought it through. I know that probably sounds dramatic, but that’s what happens.\n\nDoctor: Does this feeling of anxiety happen only when you are at work with the straight razor?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. It’s really just at work when the straight razor is involved.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound like I’m scared of every sharp thing, because I’m not. I mean, I trained with it, and I can use clippers all day without thinking twice. But when that straight razor is open at my station, it just hits me like… what if I hurt the person in the chair, or cut myself badly? I know that probably sounds extreme, but that’s the situation where it happens.\n\nDoctor: Does the thought of using a straight razor outside of work, such as at home, cause you the same level of anxiety?\n\nPatient: I guess… not really the same, no. I don’t really use one at home, so it’s hard to compare.\n\nIt’s more when it’s open at my station, with a person sitting there, that my mind goes to that place — like I’m about to cut them or cut myself badly. I know it’s just a tool, and I was trained on it, so I feel a little embarrassed saying it like that… but that’s when it feels intense.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to use a straight razor in a non-work setting, such as shaving a friend?\n\nPatient: I think… yeah, probably, if it was actually a straight razor and I was shaving a friend. Maybe not exactly the same as work, but I’d still get that feeling.\n\nI know it’s just a tool, and I was trained with it. I can use clippers all day and it doesn’t bother me. But when the straight razor is open and there’s a person there, I start feeling like I’m about to hurt them or cut myself badly. I know that might sound like I’m overreacting, but that’s honestly where my mind goes.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about using a straight razor be less if you were no longer working as a barber?\n\nPatient: Probably, yeah… I mean, I don’t want it to sound like I just can’t handle my job or something.\n\nBut I think if I wasn’t working as a barber, I wouldn’t be around an open straight razor at my station like that. That’s the part that gets me. I know it’s just a tool I was trained to use, and I can use clippers all day without any problem, but when the straight razor is sitting there open, it feels like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly.\n\nSo… yeah, I think it would be less if I wasn’t in that situation.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel anxious about using a straight razor if you were in a completely different role or situation where you had to use it, but it wasn't related to barber work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I probably would, if I actually had to use it on someone.\n\nIt’s not so much the barber shop itself, I guess. I mean, I know it’s just a tool, and I was trained with it, and I can use clippers all day without thinking twice. But with the straight razor, when it’s open and there’s a person there, my mind kind of jumps to, like… I’m going to hurt them, or I’m going to cut myself badly.\n\nI know that sounds dramatic, maybe, but that’s the part that makes me anxious.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to use a straight razor on yourself, rather than on another person?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I would, at least some.\n\nMaybe it wouldn’t be exactly the same as doing it on another person, because then I’m worried about them too, you know? But if it’s an open straight razor and I’m using it on myself, I still get that thought like I could cut myself badly.\n\nI know it’s just a tool, and I was trained with it, so I feel a little embarrassed saying that. Clippers don’t bother me at all. It’s really that open straight razor that makes me tense.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety triggered by the act of shaving itself, or is it more about the straight razor being present?\n\nPatient: It’s more the straight razor being there, I think… not shaving in general.\n\nI mean, I can use clippers all day and I don’t really think about it. And I know the razor is just a tool I trained with, so I feel kind of silly saying it, but when it’s sitting open at my station, that’s when I get tense. It feels like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly.\n\nSo yeah… it’s not the act of shaving so much as that open straight razor.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your anxiety about the straight razor would decrease if you were able to master using it again and feel fully competent with it?\n\nPatient: Maybe it would help a little if I felt more confident again, yeah… I don’t want to say it wouldn’t.\n\nBut honestly, I already know it’s just a tool, and I was trained to use it. I can use clippers all day without thinking twice. It’s just when that straight razor is open and sitting there at my station, I get this feeling like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly.\n\nSo… I’d like to think mastering it would make it go away, but I’m not completely sure it’s only about skill, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety about the straight razor more about the possibility of it causing harm, rather than the act of using it itself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably right.\n\nIt’s not really shaving itself that gets me. I know it’s just a tool, and I was trained with it, so I almost feel like I shouldn’t be making such a big deal of it. But when the straight razor is open and sitting there at my station, my mind goes to, like… what if I hurt somebody, or what if I cut myself badly?\n\nSo yeah, I’d say it’s more about what it could do, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about the straight razor be present if you were to encounter it in a completely unrelated and non-threatening situation, such as seeing one in a museum?\n\nPatient: I think… probably not as much, if it was just in a museum behind glass or something.\n\nI mean, I know it’s just a tool, and I trained with it. It’s really just at work when the straight razor is involved.\n\nI don’t want to make it sound like I’m scared of every sharp thing, because I’m not. I mean, I trained with it, and I can use clippers all day without thinking twice. But when that straight razor is open at my station, it just hits me like… what if I hurt the person in the chair, or cut myself badly? I know that probably sounds extreme, but that’s the situation where it happens.\n\nDoctor: Does the thought of using a straight razor outside of work, such as at home, cause you the same level of anxiety?\n\nPatient: I guess… not really the same, no. I don’t really use one at home, so it’s hard to compare.\n\nIt’s more when it’s open at my station, with a person sitting there, that my mind goes to that place — like I’m about to cut them or cut myself badly. I know it’s just a tool, and I was trained on it, so I feel a little embarrassed saying it like that… but that’s when it feels intense.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to use a straight razor in a non-work setting, such as shaving a friend?\n\nPatient: I think… yeah, probably, if it was actually a straight razor and I was shaving a friend. Maybe not exactly the same as work, but I’d still get that feeling.\n\nI know it’s just a tool, and I was trained with it. I can use clippers all day and it doesn’t bother me. But when the straight razor is open and there’s a person there, I start feeling like I’m about to hurt them or cut myself badly. I know that might sound like I’m overreacting, but that’s honestly where my mind goes.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about using a straight razor be less if you were no longer working as a barber?\n\nPatient: Probably, yeah… I mean, I don’t want it to sound like I just can’t handle my job or something.\n\nBut I think if I wasn’t working as a barber, I wouldn’t be around an open straight razor at my station like that. That’s the part that gets me. I know it’s just a tool I was trained to use, and I can use clippers all day without any problem, but when the straight razor is sitting there open, it feels like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly.\n\nSo… yeah, I think it would be less if I wasn’t in that situation.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would feel anxious about using a straight razor if you were in a completely different role or situation where you had to use it, but it wasn't related to barber work?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I probably would, if I actually had to use it on someone.\n\nIt’s not so much the barber shop itself, I guess. I mean, I know it’s just a tool, and I was trained with it, and I can use clippers all day without thinking twice. But with the straight razor, when it’s open and there’s a person there, my mind kind of jumps to, like… I’m going to hurt them, or I’m going to cut myself badly.\n\nI know that sounds dramatic, maybe, but that’s the part that makes me anxious.\n\nDoctor: Would you feel anxious if you were to use a straight razor on yourself, rather than on another person?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think I would, at least some.\n\nMaybe it wouldn’t be exactly the same as doing it on another person, because then I’m worried about them too, you know? But if it’s an open straight razor and I’m using it on myself, I still get that thought like I could cut myself badly.\n\nI know it’s just a tool, and I was trained with it, so I feel a little embarrassed saying that. Clippers don’t bother me at all. It’s really that open straight razor that makes me tense.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety triggered by the act of shaving itself, or is it more about the straight razor being present?\n\nPatient: It’s more the straight razor being there, I think… not shaving in general.\n\nI mean, I can use clippers all day and I don’t really think about it. And I know the razor is just a tool I trained with, so I feel kind of silly saying it, but when it’s sitting open at my station, that’s when I get tense. It feels like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly.\n\nSo yeah… it’s not the act of shaving so much as that open straight razor.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your anxiety about the straight razor would decrease if you were able to master using it again and feel fully competent with it?\n\nPatient: Maybe it would help a little if I felt more confident again, yeah… I don’t want to say it wouldn’t.\n\nBut honestly, I already know it’s just a tool, and I was trained to use it. I can use clippers all day without thinking twice. It’s just when that straight razor is open and sitting there at my station, I get this feeling like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly.\n\nSo… I’d like to think mastering it would make it go away, but I’m not completely sure it’s only about skill, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety about the straight razor more about the possibility of it causing harm, rather than the act of using it itself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably right.\n\nIt’s not really shaving itself that gets me. I know it’s just a tool, and I was trained with it, so I almost feel like I shouldn’t be making such a big deal of it. But when the straight razor is open and sitting there at my station, my mind goes to, like… what if I hurt somebody, or what if I cut myself badly?\n\nSo yeah, I’d say it’s more about what it could do, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about the straight razor be present if you were to encounter it in a completely unrelated and non-threatening situation, such as seeing one in a museum?\n\nPatient: I think… probably not as much, if it was just in a museum behind glass or something.\n\nI mean, I know it’s just a tool, and I trained with it. It’s more when it’s sitting open at my station, where I’d actually be using it near someone, that I get that feeling like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly.\n\nSo… seeing one somewhere unrelated might make me a little uncomfortable, maybe, but the real anxiety is when it’s open and right there in my work space. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about the straight razor be the same if you were to use it in a work-like setting, but not actually at your job as a barber?\n\nPatient: I think… yeah, it would probably still happen if it felt like the same kind of setup.\n\nLike, I don’t think it’s only the shop itself. I know the razor is just a tool, and I was trained with it, and I can use clippers all day without any problem. But if I’m in a work-like setting and that straight razor is open and right there, it still feels like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly.\n\nMaybe it wouldn’t be exactly as intense if it wasn’t my actual job, but… I think the fear would still be there. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about the straight razor be significantly reduced if you were to use it in a completely different context, such as a hobby, and not in a professional setting?\n\nPatient: Maybe somewhat, if it felt less serious or less like I was responsible for someone in the chair.\n\nBut I think the main thing is still the straight razor being open and right there. I know it’s just a tool, and I was trained with it, and I can use clippers all day without getting like this. But with the straight razor, even if it wasn’t technically at work, I think I’d still get that feeling like… what if I hurt somebody, or cut myself badly?\n\nSo I guess the professional setting might make it worse, but I’m not sure it would go away just because it was a hobby. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about the straight razor be the same if you were using it for a personal task at home, like shaving yourself?\n\nPatient: I think at home shaving myself, it might be a little different… maybe less pressure than having a client there.\n\nBut honestly, if it’s the straight razor and it’s open in my hand, I think I’d still get that same kind of jolt. I know it’s just a tool, and I did train with it, and I can use clippers all day without thinking twice. But with that razor, it’s like my mind goes straight to, “What if I cut myself badly?” or, if someone else is involved, “What if I hurt them?”\n\nSo maybe not exactly the same as at my station, but I don’t think I’d be totally fine with it either. Is that… is that the kind of distinction you’re asking about?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. It feels like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly.\n\nSo yeah… it’s not the act of shaving so much as that open straight razor.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your anxiety about the straight razor would decrease if you were able to master using it again and feel fully competent with it?\n\nPatient: Maybe it would help a little if I felt more confident again, yeah… I don’t want to say it wouldn’t.\n\nBut honestly, I already know it’s just a tool, and I was trained to use it. I can use clippers all day without thinking twice. It’s just when that straight razor is open and sitting there at my station, I get this feeling like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly.\n\nSo… I’d like to think mastering it would make it go away, but I’m not completely sure it’s only about skill, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Is your anxiety about the straight razor more about the possibility of it causing harm, rather than the act of using it itself?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think that’s probably right.\n\nIt’s not really shaving itself that gets me. I know it’s just a tool, and I was trained with it, so I almost feel like I shouldn’t be making such a big deal of it. But when the straight razor is open and sitting there at my station, my mind goes to, like… what if I hurt somebody, or what if I cut myself badly?\n\nSo yeah, I’d say it’s more about what it could do, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about the straight razor be present if you were to encounter it in a completely unrelated and non-threatening situation, such as seeing one in a museum?\n\nPatient: I think… probably not as much, if it was just in a museum behind glass or something.\n\nI mean, I know it’s just a tool, and I trained with it. It’s more when it’s sitting open at my station, where I’d actually be using it near someone, that I get that feeling like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly.\n\nSo… seeing one somewhere unrelated might make me a little uncomfortable, maybe, but the real anxiety is when it’s open and right there in my work space. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about the straight razor be the same if you were to use it in a work-like setting, but not actually at your job as a barber?\n\nPatient: I think… yeah, it would probably still happen if it felt like the same kind of setup.\n\nLike, I don’t think it’s only the shop itself. I know the razor is just a tool, and I was trained with it, and I can use clippers all day without any problem. But if I’m in a work-like setting and that straight razor is open and right there, it still feels like I’m about to hurt somebody or cut myself badly.\n\nMaybe it wouldn’t be exactly as intense if it wasn’t my actual job, but… I think the fear would still be there. Is that what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about the straight razor be significantly reduced if you were to use it in a completely different context, such as a hobby, and not in a professional setting?\n\nPatient: Maybe somewhat, if it felt less serious or less like I was responsible for someone in the chair.\n\nBut I think the main thing is still the straight razor being open and right there. I know it’s just a tool, and I was trained with it, and I can use clippers all day without getting like this. But with the straight razor, even if it wasn’t technically at work, I think I’d still get that feeling like… what if I hurt somebody, or cut myself badly?\n\nSo I guess the professional setting might make it worse, but I’m not sure it would go away just because it was a hobby. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Would your anxiety about the straight razor be the same if you were using it for a personal task at home, like shaving yourself?\n\nPatient: I think at home shaving myself, it might be a little different… maybe less pressure than having a client there.\n\nBut honestly, if it’s the straight razor and it’s open in my hand, I think I’d still get that same kind of jolt. I know it’s just a tool, and I did train with it, and I can use clippers all day without thinking twice. But with that razor, it’s like my mind goes straight to, “What if I cut myself badly?” or, if someone else is involved, “What if I hurt them?”\n\nSo maybe not exactly the same as at my station, but I don’t think I’d be totally fine with it either.
